## W: wentletrap–wild tobacco

### wentletrap
  
noun. a univalve shellfish (also known as curly)  
> **POWELL** CURLY or WENTLE-TRAP (<i>Cirsotrema zelebori</i>). A charming little white shell up to an inch in length with a long tapered spire sculptured with regular vertical ridges crossed by finer spirals. They wash ashore on sandy beaches of the east coast, particularly in the Bay of Plenty. *(1947,Native Animals of NZ,28)*

### wenua
  
noun.  
See: whenua

### were you born in a tent?
  
phrase. said of, or addressed as a question to, a person who leaves a door or window open.  
> **ANDERSON & AITKEN** Please, when we leave the door open they say, `Hey were you born in a tent'. *(1965,Speech and Idiom of Maori Children,96)*  
> **McGILL** <i>were you born in a tent?</i> sarcastic question of somebody who has left the door open and let draughts in. NZA. *(1988,Dictionary of Catch Phrases,122)*

### weri
  
noun. Maori for centipede  
> **BUCK** The insects [sc. in the Mangara canoe] were the weri (centipede), whe (caterpillar).. and others. *(1949,Coming of the Maori, The,61)*  
> Splitting firewood in the afn. [sic] I found a large fine centipede - a weri - body brown, with articulations very plainly marked, legs a pale blue ...[sic] *(Oct 20, 1860,Richmond-Atkinson Papers,1/645)* [NOTES: (A.S. Atkinson)]  
>  *(Sep 14, 1963,Evening Post,20)*  
> <i> weri n. </i>the vegetable caterpillar q.v. [Maori] [?error HWO] *(1929,Whitcombe's Etym. Dict. Aust-NZ Suppl.,12)*

### wero
  
noun. [Maori] a (customary Maori) challenge  
> And it was a bit of a wero, a bit of a challenge, so I did it. *(Jan 22, 2006,Sunday Star Times,16)*  
> Walking on to the marae, they receive a challenge (wero) from the local warrior. *(Dec 19, 1999,NZ Catholic,7)*  
> ...each book that he writes is like a taiaha flung quivering into the ground in front of the reader - a wero, a challenge, an eye-balling demand to hear what he has to say. *(Nov 16, 2002,Listener (New Zealand Listener),19)*  
> [They] raise valid concerns about the use of the haka and the wero. ..I hope that any foreign manuhiri involved in a wero challenge would be better informed than those two New Zealanders. *(Jun 25, 2005,Listener (New Zealand Listener),6)*  
> Sir Edmund Hillary bends his huge frame to take up the wero and makes the reply to host kaumatua Ritchie Akapita. *(Dec 24, 1990,Listener (New Zealand Listener),118)*  
> He told the Herald he was angry that Mr Sheffers smirked and looked around during his wero (challenge) to the group. *(Jan 17, 2006,NZ Herald,1)*

noun.

### West Coast
  
adjective.

### West Coast
  
noun. in New Zealand usually the west coast of the South Island.  
See: also Coast  
[NOTES: also Coast		(preceded by the)]  
> A member of our staff obtained some information bearing on the natural resources of the West Coast Sounds [sc. Fiordland]. *(May 1, 1890,Otago Witness,Suppl. 2)*  
> Floods on West Coast. *(Jan 26, 1893,Thames Star,1)*  
> The total amount of coal imported from Newcastle as a result of the prolonged mining dispute on the West Coast, according to figures supplied by the Minister of Labour .. is 192,697 tons in the last three or four months. *(Nov 15, 1923,Evening Post,5)*  
> During the night, it rained as it can rain on the West Coast when it likes. *(Dec 23, 1890,Otago Witness,17)*  
> A flourishing little port on the West Coast struck my fancy. *(Dec 23, 1890,Otago Witness,17)*  
> There are also excellent photographs of gold-sluicing on the West Coast, and many others. *(Sep 27, 1939,Rodney and Otamatea Times,3)*  
> On the West Coast we were in a new world. *(Sep 24, 1887,Auckland Weekly News,28)*  
> [Hdg] Mining Reserves on the West Coast Line. *(Mar 13, 1890,Otago Witness,14)*  
> This proves conclusively that the Notornis still survives in the secret recesses of these dense West Coast forests. *(Apr 3, 1890,Otago Witness,18)*

### West Coast death
  
noun. drowning  
[NOTES: cf. New Zealand death]  
> In the 19th century, drowning was the most common cause of accidental death among white settlers, naive to the perils of New Zealand's mercurial coast and rivers. They called it simply the West Coast death. *(Jul 12, 2004,TV1)*

### West Coast Forest Accord
  
noun.  
> What about the West Coast Accord signed in 1986, a legally binding agreement between the Labour Government (on behalf of the Crown), the West Coast United Council (united local governments), Native Forests Action Council (on behalf of various conservation groups), Royal Forest and Bird Protection Society, Federated Mountain Clubs, West Coast Timber Association and the Westland Timber Workers' Union?... <i>Ibid.</i> 87 Furthermore, she says, the present government has not breached the West Coasty Accord because it only allowed for sustainable logging and Timberlands' plan is not sustainable: "It's commercial and it hasn't been trialled." *(Apr 2000,North & South,82)*  
> Legal action trying to stop a Bill on the West Coast Forest Accord becoming law will be considered next week by Parliament's privileges committee. *(Jul 26, 2000,Evening Post,34)*  
> The 1986 West Coast Accord is the only other potential source of legal action. *(May 20, 2000,Listener (New Zealand Listener),24)*  
> We did not imagine, when we signed the West Coast Forests Accord with the Crown 14 years ago, that subsequent governments would fail to honour it. *(May 13, 2000,Listener (New Zealand Listener),7)*  
> The highlight of his career was the forest accord: logging of native timber in New Zealand will all but cease by 2002. *(Oct 20, 2000,Evening Post,12)*

### West Coast green gecko
  
noun. intricately patterned gecko, well camouflaged amongst mossy rocks and shrubs of habitat in northern Westland.  
[NOTES: <i>Naultinus tuberculatus</i> aka Mossy gecko]  
> Two species found or presumed to occur in TWCL Working Circles, West Coast green gecko and speckled skink, are listed on the IUCN Species Survival Commission 'Red List' as species of 'lower risk' but are not listed for conservation priority by DoC . . . *(Dec 1999,Internet - see Notes)* [NOTES: http://www.fore.canterbury.ac.nz/euan/beech/bucknghm.htm]  
> West Coast green geckos are also known from the area, and anunidentified brown skink was seen in March 1997 in tussock at Happy valley . . . *(2008,Internet - see Notes)* [NOTES: http://www.wcrc.govt.nz/stocktonplateau/Application/Appendix%20G%20Review%20Terrestrial%20Fauna%20-%20Ngakawau,%20Millerton,%20Stock/HDL%20Review%20of%20Terrestrial%20Fauna.pdf]  
> The West Coast green gecko is endemic to New Zealand, found only at Lewis Pass, Rahu, Reefton, and northern Westland, in the South Island. *(2009,Internet - see Notes)* [NOTES: http://www.freebase.com/view/en/west_coast_green_gecko]

### West Coast kiwi
  
noun. <i>Apterix oweni</i>  
> **HARPER** The West Coast kiwi (<i>Apterix oweni</i>) is a small grey bird, differing from the North Island species (<i>Apterix mantelli</i>), which is dark brown and more coarsely made. *(1896,Pioneer Work in the Alps of NZ,39)*

### West Coast lantern
  
noun. a candle in the neck of an upturned bottle with the base broken off (cf. bush-, manrope lantern, joey)

The miners and other friends were waiting to give us a reception, which was a [40] real West Coast one, and everybody knows what that means. *(1914,Diggers' Story, The,39)* [NOTES: 1913 Dec 10 Mrs Janie Seddon in 1914 Pfaff The Diggers' Story 39-40]

noun.

noun.

### West Coaster
  
noun. a native or resident of the West Coast  
[NOTES: see also Coaster]

> DEATH OF AN OLD WEST COASTER. *(Aug 2, 1911,Evening Post,8)*

> **HEINZ** I saw a ghastly array of departed and altogether forgotten West Coasters on this stormy night off Paringa. Richardson Rae Pencillings by Land and Sea in 1974 Heinz Bright Fine Gold 125]

> **HARPER** ... even the oldest West Coaster will watch the mad career of the river, ... *(1896,Pioneer Work in the Alps of NZ,134)*  
> He was ... not a native born West Coaster. *(1896,NZ Journal of Agriculture,2/9/157)* [NOTES: (M. Ross)]  
> **McMURRAN** The West-Coasters are proud of their parliamentary product. *(1904,From New York to NZ,81)*  
> **PFAFF** [Title] The diggers' story, or, Tales and reminiscences of the Golden Coast, from Westland's earliest pioneers. By Carl J. Pfaff. Issued by the West Coasters' Association to commemorate the Wetland Goldfields Jubilee and the unveiling of the pioneers' memorial. > **HOLLAND** A West Coaster refused to do `fire-drill' ... *(1919,Armageddon or Calvary,115)*

> **DUFF** The people are never `Southlanders' (except once a year at football) as the people of the West Coast are `West Coasters'. *(1941,New Zealand Now,71)*  
> **SMITH** ... the real West Coaster is always ready to start a party at any hour of the day even if it means interfering with breakfast. *(1947,From N to Z,74)*  
> **McLINTOCK** Other social characteristics of the population are less easily defined, but the widely accepted image of the `West Coaster' depicts a product of a close-knit community, generous, friendly, and self-reliant, yet distrustful of authority. Westland's many expatriates have undoubtedly fostered this image. *(1966,Encyclopaedia of New Zealand, An,3/635)*  
> **SEDDON** We were formed into territorial units. I found myself with my fellow West Coasters, commanding a platoon of boys from Hokitika and other parts of Westland - ... *(1968,Seddons, The,232)*  
> I am an old traveler on the West Coast, but in all my travels I never was in such a storm as that of the 12th September, when on the steamer Lyttelton, off Cape Farewell Spit .. [Signed] AN OLD WEST COASTER. *(Sep 25, 1866,Nelson Examiner and New Zealand Chronicle,3)*  
> A WEST COASTER'S WELCOME IN CANTERBURY. *(Dec 31, 1920,Grey River Argus,5)*  
> **HENDERSON** "A brow-beaten stunted lot," you maystart to think, when a giant, usuallysomeone in authority, appears, to thundersubversion and revolt beyond any WestCoaster's nigglings. *(1983,Down from Marble Mountain,270)*  
> [Caption] An Old West Coaster. *(Aug 3, 1922,Auckland Weekly News,40)*

### West Island, the
  
noun. Australia - the 'island' West of NZ  
> Living in Australia and drinking a good deal of the local reds, one soon hungers for a decent Pinot Noir or a Cabernet blend with finesse and balance... New Zealand makes better Pinot Noir, Merlot, Chardonnay, Riesling and Sparklings than any in the West Island. *(Mar 2000,Cuisine)*

### West Side
  
noun. the West Coast  
> **MONRO** On the west coast of the Middle Island, commonly called by the whalers the `West Side,' we heard a good deal both from the whites and the natives. All accounts agreed that it is of a most rugged and inaccessible character. <i>Ibid.</i> 261 The kivi, called by the sealers the emu, is met with in great abundance on the west side. It is a common article of food with them, being caught with the assistance of dogs. It seems likely that there are two species of kivi, one much larger than the other. *(Jul 20, 1844,Notes of a Journey)* [NOTES: 1844 Monro Notes of a Journey (Nelson <i>Examiner</i> July 20 et seq.) in 1898 Hocken Contributions 260 1845 NZ Journal V/149/234 (David Monro) [quot. as above???]]

### Westcargot
  
noun. [humorous] a West Coast snail  
> The schools who put up their tents, the guides who collect garden snails for their Westcargo food stall, the shopkeepers who dress their windows and stay open 24 hours, all turn a blind eye to the brief bacchanalian revelry. *(Mar 13, 2001,Evening Post,22)*  
> The Japanese said they would not be able to attend the festival.. meaning they would miss out on other local delicacies such as earthworms, worm slammers (in glasses of moonshine whisky), and "Westcargots" (garden snails). *(Mar 6, 2002,Dominion,2)*  
> The festival is where.. the brave can venture into a culinary no man's land of horse meat, huhu grubs, snails (Westcargot to the locals), crouching grasshoppers with peanut sauce, mountain oysters.. and whitebait patties - all to be washed down with West Coast ales, billy tea, moonshine or wines. *(Mar 3, 2002,Sunday Star Times,F5)*

### Western Bank
  
noun. the western embankment of (the former) Athletic Park rugby stadium in Wellington  
> The mists coming down on the Western bank, just before the final whistle blows. *(Jan 24, 1944,NZEF Times,III/135 : 11)*  
> Then on to one of the trams, already filling with rugby regulars that trundled up through Newtown to the Park. Once through the park gate we were out on to the empry, waiting, glorious green field, and running and climbing up to our favourite position (our own by a divine right I never questioned), right above the halfway line, way up high on the Western Bank... even today, I only have to close my eyes and I am back with Dad on our own high place on the Western Bank. *(Jun 2003,NZ Memories,No. 42/71)*  
> I am sure all my contemporaries willrecall.. hitching their way intoWellington to get into one of thoseseemingly never-ending queues for the"Western Bank". *(1981,St Patrick's College, Silverstream 1931-1980,27)*

### Westie
  
noun. [informal] a West Coaster  
> We might next week just wander in and see what they do with the Westies' whitebait. *(Sep 17, 2005,Dominion Post,E8)*

### Westie
  
noun. person from the western suburbs of Auckland  
> Petrol heads, bogans, and gruffs - these names mean one thing to west Auckland comedian Ewen Gilmour : "westies". Gilmour believes westies live all over the country. No matter where they live they share a love of tight black jeans, the smell of burning rubber and the feel of a leather jacket. Being a westie is about lifestyle, not location. *(Jun 15, 1998,Evening Post,TV Week / 15)*  
> No one will ever know just how manybogans - "Wellington westies" - lie onthe ocean floor stretching out beyondParaparaumu beach (having failed in theirintoxicated attempts to swim out toKapiti wearing upward of two pairs ofblack jeans). *(Apr 29, 1996,Dominion,21)*  
> So what exactly is a Westie? "I think youcall us Bogans down in Wellington... A call to Harry Orsman.. reveals that'bogan' is in fact of Australian ratherthan Wellington origin. "It is a nerd ora gormless bugger. Someone who is a bitstupid or brainless". *(Apr 24, 1996,City Voice,5)*  
> "And what about the Westies, the homeboys, the petrol heads...?" *(Jan 29, 1994,Listener (New Zealand Listener),8)*  
> I stood with hundred of others peacefully and rather helplessly until a Westie borstal old-girl I knew, standing near me, acting out of a combination of boredom and frustration, grabbed hold of the mesh and started to wrench it from its iron supports. *(Aug 14, 1999,Listener (New Zealand Listener),43)*  
> Gilmour's on stage persona as an Auckland westie is far removed from his real character... Marriage has taken Gilmour further away from the lifestyle of a westie. *(May 14, 2003,Capital Times,12)*  
> He did what any sensible Westie would do - collect his salary and bide his time. *(Feb 1, 1997,Listener (New Zealand Listener),30)*  
> "There's a cringe factor from advertising agencies, mainly PR women, who use the term 'westie' as a put-down," Palmer says. *(Dec 18, 1999,Listener (New Zealand Listener),31)*  
> After all, this is a film about Westies, written and directed by a Westie, designed to put the Westie play-hard go-hard subculture up on the screen. I suspect this is a sanitised version of the real thing, but there's still enough here to appeal to the Westie in all of us. (Go on, indulge your inner hoon.) *(Mar 25, 2000,Listener (New Zealand Listener),40)*  
> Aucklanders have erroneously labelled the bogan as the "Westie", failing to appreciate that Westies have been thriving in the provinces far south of the Bombay Hills for much longer than they have occupied West Auckland... "You know you're a bogan when you come home on your 15th birthday and your dad says, 'Okay son, it's time I gave you a talk about the facts of life' and you go 'Okay, Dad, hang on, I'll just put my kids to bed...'" *(May 18, 2000,Dominion,11)*  
> There was the naughty Westie girl nose wrinkle when she asked the Washington pundit, "Is Iowa really that important?" *(Jan 31, 2004,Listener (New Zealand Listener),78)*  
> 'Westie party pad' was a drug lab.[hdg] "We just thought it was a Westies' party pad," he said. *(May 9, 2007,Dominion Post,A6)*  
> The mullet, sometimes pronounced "moo-lay", is the signature cut of Westie males. They are members of a great tribe that ranges across the Anglo-Saxon world: there are comrade Westies in Wanganui, munters in Masterton, bogan hordes in the Hutt Valley,; there are yobbies and Westies in Australia, crackers and trailer trash in the US and chavs, slappers and Essex girls in the UK. *(Oct 2007,North & South,83)*  
> A Westie'll always be the firstto pop a glass of rough red in yourhand... Let's not forget New Zealand'sfirst pop guitarist came out of westAuckland... Sort the logic out and thensee who's the Westie. *(Jul 1994,Quote Unquote,11)*  
> You're going to just love Friends , thefirst network sitcom to dive into thatelusive Generation X thing (Gen X, likeWestie, is a thing everyone can identifybut no one will admit to). *(Feb 18, 1995,Listener (New Zealand Listener),67)*  
> Songwriter Jan Hellriegel sashays out ofthe Western Auckland suburbs blot with"Westy Gals." ... The song is a slow,strong-tempered memory of rage andteenage kids which Hellriegel.. wentthrough while growing up in Henderson,the great metropolis of "westies" -hoons, yobs, chicks and party animals ofnumerous vivid subcultures - who chargeinto the New Zealand night. *(Oct 3, 1992,Listener (New Zealand Listener),44)*  
> What do you call people who live on the North Shore? Westies with jobs. *(Aug 4, 2001,Listener (New Zealand Listener),23)*  
> But the Big Day Out would not be the same without the long haired, tight black-jeaned Westies... *(Jan 21, 2006,Dominion Post,A3)*  
> Carmody said she liked West Auckland for its beaches and bush, but, she admitted she didn't like to b thrown in with the Westie sterotype of a black-clad bogan. *(Sep 26, 2010,Sunday Star Times,C4)*  
> It stars Timothy Balme as wimpy Arthurwho picks up June, hitchhiker from helland a Westie to boot on the way toPalmerston North. *(May 21, 1995,Sunday Star Times,D2)*
