### whakamomori
  
noun.  
> **ZAVOS** For too long.. the Maoris had regarded the issue [sc. South African rugby tours] with whakamomori - 'suffering in silence." *(1979,After the Final Whistle,127)*  
> ... some of the Puketapu and Wi Kingi's natives have come over from the enemy as a <i>whakamomori, i.e.,</i> have come over under the impulse of vexation. *(Mar 23, 1861,Otago Witness,6)*

### whakangahau
  
noun. [Maori]  
[NOTES: whakangahau: encourage, incentive (P.M. Ryan Dict. Modern Maori (1995) 303)]  
> *Workshops  *Review policies and confirm 5 year business Plan  *Whakangahau *(Sep 29, 2006,Whakatane Beacon,18)*

### whakangungu
  
noun. [Maori] trainer, training officer  
See: -  
> Kaitohutohu/whakangungu Advisor/trainer ... The Parliamentary Service and the Office of the Clerk are seeking the services of a Kaitohutohu/whakangungu  to provide liaison and suppport services relating to the good employer responsibilities and EEO obligations under the State Sector Act. *(Nov 19, 2005,Dominion Post,H13)*

### whakanoa
  
noun.  
> ... in order to stop the ravages of the avenging spirit, it becomes necessary to abolish or make a void (whakanoa) to this superstitious observance [ie. Tapu]. *(Aug 15, 1857,Lyttelton Times,3)*

### whakapapa
  
verb. [Maori] trace or recite ancestral history  
> They were without a marae to whakapapa to and were left with a quarter acre cemetery as the last piece of land they could tribally claim as their own. *(Apr 2004,Wel-com (Wellington),5)*  
> We don't only whakapapa into tainui; we also whakapapa into Te Arawa. *(May 9, 1999,Radio NZ / National Radio,Maori business man : Spectrum)* [NOTES: Quoted from memory but essentially correct]  
> All of the people of Te Teko can whakapapa to eachother; we have always been told of this from a young age and to do your best to awhi your whanau ... *(Oct 20, 2006,Whakatane Beacon,2)*  
> "I'll tell you what - the MacDonald family from Marlborough are very, very strong Maori. Jamie Joseph whakapapa's to that family. ..." *(Jun 1, 2006,NZ Herald,C18)*  
> He fails to mention.. the fact that all tribes can whakapapa back to the first landing of the canoes more than 1000 years ago. *(Nov 5, 1992,Evening Post,6)*

### whakapapa
  
noun. [Maori] a genealogy or family tree  
> These young performers... are the latest in a remarkable whakapapa. They stand on the shoulders of a heritage of New Zealand voices - Francis Alda, Mina Foley, Inia Te Wiata, Malvina Major, Kiri te Kanawa. *(2000,Opera Voices,No. 2 : 11)*  
> There are not many local wines able to boast any sort of serious whakapapa - at least not many wines of note. *(Sep 30, 2000,Listener (New Zealand Listener),44)*  
> In my whakapapa the nannies were all strong women. *(Dec 16, 2000,Listener (New Zealand Listener),58)*  
> I feel a strong sense of belonging here, or what the Maori call "whakapapa". *(Apr 1998,North & South,79)*  
> John (who's got an Italian whakapapa) and Abe then formed JADCO to  manufacture and market the book repair kits. *(Feb 1998,Mana,No. 20: 34)*  
> For Ramari it's part of a renaissance and an opportunity to give contemporary bonecarving a whakapapa that has long been missing. *(Jun 1996,Mana,No.13: 6)*  
> Former Prime Ministers Kirk, Muldoon, Nordmeyer, Savage, Fraser and Holland were invoked as part of a whakapapa who all had controls on foreign investment. *(May 27, 1995,Listener (New Zealand Listener),16)*  
> McGregor says the most important things are whakapapa, knowing about your ancestors and roots so you can move into the future, and tikanga, "the way you interact with people, your upbringing and your culture". *(Oct 18, 2002,Dominion Post,A11)*  
> **HULME & MORRISON** When I look over that whakapapa, I know why I can stand waist-deep in winter sea, and not get chilled to the bone. *(1989,Homeplaces,60)*  
> When you're dealing with whakapapa, it's a taonga, it's precious. *(Aug 14, 1999,Listener (New Zealand Listener),33)*  
> Antinori wines sit comfortably on the most salubrious wine lists in Europe and North America where they command prices in keeping with the Marchesi Piero Antinori's 600-year whakapapa in the wine business. *(Oct 9, 1999,Listener (New Zealand Listener),57)*  
> It doesn't ruin the story to say that, among the two or three things Dorothy Allison knows for sure, is that we must know where we come from - our whakapapa - and that by telling our stories we change the world. *(Sep 7, 1996,Listener (New Zealand Listener),44)*  
> Simpler and more romantic is the Maori whakapapa which records the ti as descending over about 15 generations from Ranginui, the Sky Father, and Papatuanuku, Earth Mother. *(Jun 2, 2001,Dominion,14)*  
> **ANDERSON** Miri tells me that he is finding his ancestors, checking up on his whakapapa he calls it. *(1991,Long Hot Summer,21)*  
> The new black hole rang with the cheerful din of empathetically bonded bureaucrats reciting their whakapapa. *(Aug 7, 1999,Listener (New Zealand Listener),13)*  
> You can tell them that our whakapapa (ancestry) is our land title,. our ownership papers... *(Apr 6, 2003,Sunday Star Times,A6)*  
> Coleraine's whakapapa gave a display that made the point that top-quality wine was not about volume, but about grace, that fine sense of balance and breadth that is the dominant characteristic of the world's best reds, and that it is possible to do that in New Zealand. *(Aug 7, 1999,Listener (New Zealand Listener),57)*  
> .. a prestigious event for the Arawa subtribe, which maintains strong whakapapa and tribal links to the King Movement. *(Sep 26, 2006,NZ Herald,A3)*  
> Eddie's whakapapa is represented by a small tree that he seems to plant and uproot several times throughout the film. *(Sep 21, 1996,Listener (New Zealand Listener),44)*  
> Muaka Te Hei endeavoured to establish a claim to the same estate, but failed to prove his whakapapa (descent), and his objection was therefore not sustained. *(Jul 6, 1886,Waikato Times,2)*  
> In order to prove certain rights by succession, the Natives of the Maori Land Court produce a whakapapa, or family tree, which shows their parents, grand parents, and so on back for generations. *(Feb 25, 1910,Ohinemuri Gazette,3)*  
> Whakapapa is a comprehensible paradigm of reality which traces the origin of the universe, the genesis of gods, the creation of woman, the rise of the legendary heroes who established cultural norms and social institutions, the ancestors who migrated from Hawaiki to New Zealand and the heroic ancestors who demarcated tribal territory and established tribal polities which remained extant into historic times. Whakapapa were used to validate tribal mana and claims to the land, functions which were properly recognised by the Native Land Court. *(Jan 21, 1991,Listener (New Zealand Listener),85)* [NOTES: Ranginui Walker]  
> White people too need  a sense of whakapapa and a place to stand. *(Feb 1992,Metro,118)*  
> **TREGEAR** The recital of genealogies (<i> whaka papa </i> ) was sometimes assisted by the use of a notched or carved piece of wood (<i> rakau whakapapa </i> ) ... *(1904,Maori Race, The,384)*  
> Throughout the Matorohanga and Nepia Pohuhu matter are references and whakapapa relating to Hinekauirangi and Hakirirangi. *(Jan 11, 1930,Na To Hoa Aroha,2/98)* [NOTES: Ngata]  
> **WAITE** To check the foregoing, here is Tuhawaiki's own Whakapapa (family tree) as dictated by him to Shortland in 1844 ... *(1940,Port Molyneux,35)*  
> **HILLIARD** `You don't know them yourself! You don't even know your own <i> whakapapa!' </i> <i>Ibid..</i> 192  <i> whakapapa </i>  ancestry *(1963,Piece of Land, A,18)*  
>  *(1966,Short Stories (various authors))* [NOTES: 1960 Hilliard in 1966 Short Stories]  
> **RAWLINSON** They quarrelled ... / about landrights and whakapapas,/ ... / about *(1963,Of Clouds and Pebbles,23)*  
> **MASON** HINGAWARU ... But I remember my grandfather. Spoke not a word of English, never, till he died. Full of waiata and karakia; taught me the whakapapa right back to Hoturoa and the Tainui. *(1974,Hand on the Rail, The,18)*  
> **REED** A study of various whakapapa casts a great deal of doubt on such an assumption, for there may have been as many as three Kupes. *(1977,Treasury of Maori Exploration,21)*  
> **WILSON** If a strange new `birder' arrived on an island he `spoke' his whakapapa (family tree) and if his claim was genuine, he was welcomed. *(1979,Titi Heritage,45)*  
> Chris believes that knowing one's ancestry is vitally important and he has had his complete genealogy drawn up. He nods toward the dark interior of the meeting house. `I couldn't get up in there and recite my whakapapa -...' *(Feb 17, 1982,Dominion,9)*  
> **HULME** 106 Whakapapa = genealogies, family trees *(1983,Bone People, The,466)*  
> **HOWE** Whakapapa - `genealogy'. One is one's history. Therefore knowledge of one's family tree is more than an academic exercise - it enables one to link with one's everpresent ancestors. *(1985,Towards Taha Maori,24)*  
> **IHIMAERA** The child was listening to the matriarch telling him of his ancestry, his whakapapa. *(1986,Matriarch, The,1)*  
> **HULME** She's proud of that whakapapa. As she's one of the blue-eyed white-blond kind of Maori, she probably feels she needs it, nei? *(1986,Te Kaihau,92)*  
> The whakapapas preserved are those of rangatira lines, which practically obliterated existing genealogies. ... you must deny the latter any whakapapa system. *(Mar 22, 1933,Na To Hoa Aroha,3/73)* [NOTES: (Ngata)]  
> When gospel writers later traced Jesus' whakapapa, the name of Jesse ("God exists") appears on it. *(Nov 9, 2002,Dominion Post,A13)*  
> 'Nana, I want to know about your mother and where she is from, I want to know my whakapapa.' *(2007,Huia 7: Contemp. Maori Fiction,65)*  
> True, Harrier Rise does have a couple of labels straight out of Europe, but, then, wine has a whakapapa, too, and there is something as visceral in a red Alfa Romeo as there is in getting wine from your local soil. *(Jan 19, 2002,Listener (New Zealand Listener),44)*  
> **DIEFFENBACH** The banks of the Keri-keri are also the only known habitat of the elegant Clianthus puniceus, a leguminous plant, which the natives appropriately call parrot's-bill - ko-waingutu kaka. *(1843,Travels in NZ,1/250)*  
> If you know nothing about your lineage and are interested in tracing your whakapapa, the internet is the place to start. *(Nov 12, 2003,Capital Times,4)*  
> Barossa with Burge is one of the great wine journeys, filled with the sort of history that is stuffed to the brim with people and vivid yarns, with vineyard whakapapa and the whiff of wine wrung out ot a dusty landscape. *(May 25, 2002,Listener (New Zealand Listener),44)*  
> .. a dark-eyed wine with a whakapapa that runs back to Bordeaux and further into the mysterious hills of Spain. *(Aug 20, 2005,Listener (New Zealand Listener),59)*  
> The Brierley tribe has a proud whakapapa. *(Oct 17, 1992,Listener (New Zealand Listener),37)*

### whakapapa staff
  
noun.  
> In the popular sense a taonga is a highly prized possession to which is attached korero (text or story). An example is a whakapapa (genealogy) staff. *(1984,Te Maori,22)*

### whakapapa tapu
  
noun. [Maori] sacred genealogy  
> But before the geneophiles can extract my whakapapataapuu (sacred gene bank) from me, I want to know, who owns what? *(Aug 10, 2005,NZ Herald,A16)*

### whakapiopio
  
noun.  
> **HOOKER** Whakapiopio, <i>Lindsay.</i> Metrosideros <i>sp.Lindsay. Metrosideros scandens. </i> *(1867,Handbook NZ Flora (List of Names),769)*

### whakapohane
  
noun.  
> Mr Mihaka said while he believed he should be able to perform the whakapohane (buttock baring act) to anybody ... *(Oct 31, 1989,Dominion,3)*

### whakapono
  
noun.  
> **HILLIARD** Men have a sort of code among themselves, a whakapono. They have to show the world they're on top all the time, ... *(1960,Maori Girl,159)*

### whakariki
  
noun.  
> **LINDSAY** <i> P. Colensoi</i> is apparently what the Maoris designate `Whakariki, (syns. `Wharariki' - `Wharaeki') according to the late Bishop Williams ... and which he defines variously as `a <i>stunted kind </i>of Flax plant' ... *(1868,Contributions to NZ Botany,78)*

### whakaro
  
noun. [Maori] thought(s), idea(s), view(s)  
See: also whakaaro  
[NOTES: formed from whaka+aro; MLC recommends spelling whakaaro not whakaro (with or without macron on second a) but both are well attested on Google]  
> VOX POP will be cruising around the country, so keep your eyes open for your chance to get into <i>Taiohi</i> and share your whakaro with the other rangatahi - this time Poneke students tell us wassup. *(2003,Te Mana  Taiohi,1,Winter 2003)*

### whakarongo
  
noun. [Maori] listening  
> In the course you will learn korero (speaking), whakarongo (listening), tuhituhi (writing), panui (presentation), and language structure in a safe and comfortable whanau environment. *(Dec 2, 2005,Whakatane Beacon,10)*

### Whakatahuri steak
  
noun.  
[NOTES: ca 1984 Havelock a piece of dry white bread. (from the Pelorus Sounds bay, renowned for its rough and ready ways; and more recently for its `hippie' colony.)]

### whakatane
  
noun. [Maori] a transgendered female to male person  
> I dislike double dipping immensely. What I mean is, whakawahine or whakatane, wanting to assume the roles of BOTH genders. *(Jan 16, 2008,Internet - see Notes)*  
> I have been to a marae where a whakatane has stood to do the karanga. *(Jan 15, 2008,Internet - see Notes,1)* [NOTES: www.maori.org.nz/papa_panui/forum_topic.asp?]  
> .. a Whakatane should not in Tikanga (I definately [sic] dont [sic]) take on the role of a Tane in all its entirety nor should a Whakawahine do so as a Wahine ... *(Jan 24, 2008,Internet - see Notes)* [NOTES: www.maori.org.nz/papa_panui/forum_topic.asp?]

### Whakatane harrows
  
noun. [Rural]  
> .. timber jack, set Whakatane harrows, 2 porcelain hand bowls ... *(Nov 7, 1942,Evening Post,10)*  
> [Advt]. Get to grips with the sod boundgrass... The strong tines of the harrowsbreak up the unproductive root-mats anddestroy moss, therefore, treat yourpastures with - The Whakatane Harrows...Pulled one way these harrows give a 5-inch tine cultivation; reversed andpulled the other way, a 3-inch tripodeffect... <i>Ibid.</i> [Advt] The "Whakatane" Harrow... Out ofexperimental work of this nature has comethis new soil renovator... The tines,tripod, and to some extent the chainharrow effect have all been combined inthis set of harrows. *(May 26, 1928,NZ Dairy Produce Exporter,29)*  
> [Advt]. Superior to all Imitations.  TheWhakatane Harrow. Three Harrows in One. *(Feb 1931,Exporter & Home Journal,Front Cover)*

### Whakatane Tephra
  
noun. Geol. definition  
> On field appearance and stratigraphy, the thin tephra was considered not to be an Okataina tephra [sic, small 't'] (Kaiharoa or Whakatane Tephra) but perhaps a tephra from Taupo or the basaltic Rotokawau Ash. *(Oct 1984,Geological Society of NZ Newsletter,66/18)*

### whakatangitangi
  
noun.  
> **HOOKER** Whakatangitangi, <i>Lindsay. Metrosideros sp. </i> *(1867,Handbook NZ Flora (List of Names),769)*

### whakatata
  
noun.  
> **RICHARDS** <i>Corokia ... macrocarpa</i> ... WHAKATATA, HOKOTATA. *(1952,Chatham Islands,55)*

### whakatau / mihi whakatau
  
noun. [Maori] a Maori welcome less formal than a powhiri  
> <b>WHAKATAU</B> - LESS FORMAL MAORI WELCOMING [than powhiri] -Men and women have the same roles.-Seating arrangements reflect same roles for men and women. -Other languages used as required. *(Jan 19, 2006,Dominion Post,1)*  
> Less formal welcomes, or whakatau would be used. These assign the same roles to men and women, removing perceived sexism. *(Jan 19, 2006,Dominion Post,1)*  
> Dr Sharples .. saying the form and content of whakatau varied from iwi to iwi. *(Jan 20, 2006,Listener (New Zealand Listener),A2)*  
> The department says the "general tenor" of the advice it received from .. its own Maori staff and Te Puni Kokiri was that the whakatau allowed for similar roles for men and women. *(Feb 4, 2006,NZ Herald,22,Canvas)*  
> Corrections dropped the use of powhiri in favour of a whakatau - a less formal welcome - after the furore sparked by Josie Bullock, then a probation officer, who demanded to sit in the front row. *(May 25, 2006,NZ Herald,A1)*  
> A mihi whakatau will commence at 9.00 am. *(Dec 17, 2008,Dominion Post,D3)*  
> The Annual General Meeting of Shareholders will be held on <b>Saturday 20th November 2010</b> at Papauma Marae on Owahanga Station commencing at 9.30am with a mihi whakatau. *(Nov 6, 2010,Wairarapa Times-Age,18)*  
> I .. can remember all too well the nervousness I experienced, as a European New Zealander, when I was asked whether I would like a powhiri or a mihi whakatau to welcome me to the organisation. *(Jan 18, 2006,NZ Herald,A14)*

### whakatauki/whakatauaki
  
noun. [Maori] a proverb  
[NOTES: Williams gives both spellings (at tau sense v)]  
> "I WILL NEVER BE LOST - I AM A SEED OF RANGIATEA." THIS IS A FAVOURITE WHAKATAUKI FOR NGATI POROU PHOTO MEDIA ARTIST NATALIE ROBERTSON. Notes *(2005,Te Mana  Taiohi,issue 18 summer 2005 / 2006, p15)*  
> Even iwi representative Ken Mair, who might seem a pretty prosaic chap, gets lyrical about it, citing the old whakatauki or proverb - "I am the river and the river is me." *(Dec 14, 2002,Listener (New Zealand Listener),30)*  
> The whakatauaki or saying here is about 'the knitting of people when peace is made'. *(Nov 2003,AA Directions,58/23)*

### whakatuwhera
  
verb. [Maori] to open  
[NOTES: cf. P.M. Ryan Dict. Modern Maori (1995) 312.]  
> <b>AGENDA</b> *karakia whakatuwhera ... *(Apr 19, 2006,Whakatane Beacon,15)*

### whakawahine
  
noun. [Maori] transgendered male to female person  
> I dislike double dipping immensely. What I mean is, whakawahine or whakatane, wanting to assume the roles of BOTH genders. *(Jan 16, 2008,Internet - see Notes)* [NOTES: www.maori.org.nz/papa_panui/forum_topic.asp?]  
> The resource [ <i> Ko la - he or she </i> ] is believed to be the first in Aotearoa New Zealand produced specifically for whakawahine (Maori male to female transgender) *(Jun 1, 2005,Scoop,1)*  
> Submissions came from a wide variety of trans people (whakawahine, fa'afafine, MtFs, FtMs, fakaleiti, cross dressers, androgynous and genderqueeer people etc and some intersex people). *(2007,Internet - see Notes)* [NOTES: www.hrc.co.nz/home/hrc/introduction/actiononthetransgenderinquiry/resources/]  
> .. a Whakatane should not in Tikanga (I definately [sic] dont [sic]) take on the role of a Tane in all its entirety nor should a whakawahine do so as a Wahine ... *(Dec 10, 2007,Internet - see Notes,3)* [NOTES: www.maori.org.nz/papa_panui/forum_topic.asp?]  
> **Human Rights Commission** Pronouns in this report are based on a person's gender identity. For example, a trans woman, MtF, whakawahine or fa'afafine is referred to as 'she'; while a trans man is referred to as 'he'. *(Jan 2008,To Be Who I Am,31)*

### whakou
  
noun.  
> **McKILLOP** The tanekaha tree yields a black or brown dye, and the tuhuhi a blue-black dye from the wood and bark, as does the mako; the whakou also yields a handsome blue. *(1849,Reminiscences,162)*

### whakou
  
noun.  
> **COCKAYNE** The tawiri (<i>Ixerbia brexioides</i>) of the Auckland upland forest is so showy that the Maoris had a special name, `whakou,' for its blooms. *(1910,NZ Plants,37)*

### whale
  
noun.  
> At dawn on January 24, 285 pilot whales were stranded on Farewell Spit by the receding tide... Through the binoculars I counted a dozen pilot whales in the shallow water. *(Apr 15, 1991,Listener (New Zealand Listener),29)*

### whale
  
noun.  
> [He] said the discovery of the rare gingko-toothed beaked whale, only the second such find in New Zealand, was likely to create international interest. *(Nov 3, 2004,Dominion Post,A7)*  
> The first gingko-toothed whale stranding in New Zealand, which happened last month in New Plymouth, has scientists at the Museum of New Zealand, Te Papa, buzzing with excitement... The species had only two gingko-leaf-shaped teeth, one on either side of the lower jaw, sucked on their prey and swallowed it whole. *(May 12, 2003,Dominion Post,A9)*

### whale
  
noun.  
[NOTES: general and undefined usages]  
> Right whales have been protected since 1936, although whaling continued illegally until 1950. *(Jul 5, 2000,Evening Post,11)*  
> A characteristic of right whales is theraised patches of white, rough skin onthe snout. Called callosities, they areinfested with barnacles, parasitic wormsand whole live... Callosities have givenrise to the fishermen's name for thespecies - Barnacle Bill - given first toa friendly individual off the NapierCoast. *(Jan 2, 1993,Listener (New Zealand Listener),28)*  
> **WAKEFIELD** They [shore-stations] are fitted out for the capture of the black or <i>`right' </i> whale, [fn <i> Balaena Antipodum </i>] ...; but occasionally sperm [fn <i> Physeter Macrocephalus </i>] or hump-back [fn <i> Balaena Gibbosa </i> ] also near the land sufficiently to be taken by the shore-parties. <i>Ibid.,</i> 153 Of <i> Whales </i>, there are the <i> Sperm (Physeter Macrocephalus)</i>, the <i> Humpback (Balaena gibbosa)</i>, the <i> Fin-back (Balaena physalus)</i>, the <i> Pike-headed (Balaena Boops)</i>, the <i> Large-lipped (Balaenopterus musculus)</i>, and the <i> Black </i> or <i> Right whale (Balaena Antipodum)</i> . *(1848,Handbook for New Zealand, The,129)*  
> **POLACK** Among the larger tribes that sport in shoals in the vicinity of the shore, are, sperm-whales, called para[as-acute] <i> paraua </i>[a3-acute] (physeter macrocephalus); black whale, <i> mungu nui </i> (black physeter); right whale, <i> tohora </i> (balaena mysticetus); finback, (balaena physalis); sun-fish (diodon mola); large-lipped whale (balaena mysticetus); large-lipped whale (balaena musculus); hump-back (balaena gibbosa); shark, <i> mango </i>(squalus); hammer-head (zygaena); pilots, (scomber); *(1840,Manners & Customs of the NZers,1/202 fn)*  
> **WAKEFIELD** Of <i> Whales </i>, there are the <i> Sperm (Physeter Macrocephalus)</i>, the <i> Humpback (Balaena gibbosa), </i> the <i> Fin-back (Balaena physalus)</i>, the <i> Pike-headed (Balaena Boops)</i>, the <i> Large-lipped (Balaenopterus musculus)</i>, and the <i> Black </i> or <i> Right whale (Balaena Antipodum).</i> *(1848,Handbook for New Zealand, The,153)*  
> **WAKEFIELD** Of <i> Whales </i>, there are the <i> Sperm (Physeter Macrocephalus)</i>, the <i> Humpback (Balaena gibbosa), </i> the <i> Fin-back (Balaena physalus)</i>, the <i> Pike-headed (Balaena Boops)</i>, the <i> Large-lipped (Balaenopterus musculus)</i>, and the <i> Black </i> or <i> Right whale (Balaena Antipodum).</i> *(1848,Handbook for New Zealand, The,153)*  
> RORQUALUS (Sp.,) Trigger-fin, Razor-back, Sulphur- bottom (to be distinguished from the Finner, which is properly the <i> B. Marginata.</i> ) *(1870,TrNZI,2/25)* [NOTES: (Hector)]  
> **POLACK** Among the larger tribes that sport in shoals in the vicinity of the shore, are, sperm-whales, called <i> para[as-acute] </i> paraua <i>[a3-acute] (physeter macrocephalus); black whale, </i> mungu nui <i> (black physeter); right whale, </i> tohora <i> (balaena mysticetus); finback, (balaena physalis); sun-fish (diodon mola); large-lipped whale (balaena mysticetus); large-lipped whale (balaena musculus); hump-back (balaena gibbosa); shark, </i> mango <i>(squalus); hammer-head (zygaena); pilots, (scomber); *(1840,Manners & Customs of the NZers,1/202 fn)*  
> **WAKEFIELD** They [shore-stations] are fitted out for the capture of the black or `right' whale, [fn Balaena Antipodum] ...; but occasionally sperm [fn Physeter Macrocephalus] or hump-back [fn Balaena Gibbosa] also near the land sufficiently to be taken by the shore-parties. </i>Ibid.</i>153 Of <i> Whales </i>, there are the <i> Sperm (Physeter Macrocephalus)</i>, the <i> Humpback (Balaena gibbosa), </i> the <i> Fin-back (Balaena physalus)</i>, the <i> Pike-headed (Balaena Boops)</i>, the <i> Large-lipped (Balaenopterus musculus)</i>, and the <i> Black </i> or <i> Right whale (Balaena Antipodum).</i> *(1848,Handbook for New Zealand, The,129)*  
> **WILLIAMS** <i> Kewa </i>  (ii), n. <i> balaena autralis, right whale. Tohora,</i>  n. <i> Balaena australis, southern right whale.</i> *(1917,Dictionary of the Maori Language,135)*  
> **GRADY** <i> Right whale (Southern)</i>  - Also called black whale. <i> Balaena glacialis australis. </i> Easily recognised by its arched mouth and lack of dorsal fin. The Peranos caught a few of these in their early years of Cook Strait whaling. They are now totally protected and are rarely seen in N.Z. waters today *(1982,Perano Whalers,228)* [NOTES: [glossary].]  
> **POLACK** Among the leviathans, who sport in shoals around these shores, the para paraua [a's-acute], or <i>sperm-whale</i> (Physeter macrocephalus); the tohora, or </i>right-whale</i> (Balaena bysticoetus); the mungu nui, or <i>black physeter</i>; the</i> sun-fish</i> (Diodonmola); <i>fin-back</i> (Balaena physalis); the </i>musculus</i>, or large-lipped whale; .. *(1838,New Zealand  (Polack),1/323)*  
> **POLACK** Among the larger tribes that sport in shoals in the vicinity of the shore, are, sperm-whales, called para[as-acute] <i> paraua </i>[a3-acute] (physeter macrocephalus); black whale, <i> mungu nui </i> (black physeter); right whale, <i> tohora </i> (balaena mysticetus); finback, (balaena physalis); sun-fish (diodon mola); large-lipped whale (balaena mysticetus); large-lipped whale (balaena musculus); hump-back (balaena gibbosa); shark, <i> mango </i>(squalus); hammer-head (zygaena); pilots, (scomber); . *(1840,Manners & Customs of the NZers,1/202 fn)*  
> RORQUALUS (Sp.,) Trigger-fin, Razor-back, Sulphur- bottom (to be distinguished from the Finner, which is properly the </i> B. Marginata.<i> ) *(1870,TrNZI,2/25)* [NOTES: (Hector)]

verb. (a person or thing), to attack vigorously

noun.

### whale chasing
  
noun.  
[NOTES: whale-chasing]  
> She combined whale-chasing with trading, as many vessels did in the South Seas in those days. *(Oct 4, 1913,Marlborough Express,6)*  
> The damage was sustained while whale-chasing last summer amongst the ice-sprinkled waters of the Ross Sea. *(May 21, 1926,Evening Post,6)*  
> Whale-chasing is much more scientific than it was in the days of that schoolboy classic, "Peter the Whaler." *(Apr 30, 1932,Evening Post,19)*

### whale food
  
noun.  
See: whalefeed  
> The disturbance caused by steamers, and the failure of "whale food" are also feasible theories, but their verification is confessedly difficult. *(Apr 27, 1870,Otago Daily Times,4)*  
> Picton harbor has been gorgeous during the past few days with animalcul, commonly called whale food. *(Oct 27, 1896,Marlborough Express,2)*  
> So much whale food has come ashore on the beach in certain parts of the district (says the North Otago Times) that farmers are carting it on to their land as a manure. *(Jan 31, 1914,Poverty Bay Herald,6)*  
> The boys wanted to know about boats, guns, bombs, how big is a big whale, and so on, and the girls about whale food, whale mothers and babies, and whale family affairs generally. *(May 30, 1930,Evening Post,8)*  
> Lieutenant-Commander Marr was senior zoologist on one of the five 20-month cruises made before the war by the latter ship investigating the prevalence of plankton and krill (whale food) on the ocean and marking the whales with darts to discover their habits and the best methods of preservation. *(Nov 14, 1944,Evening Post,3)*  
> **McNAB** >2. <i>Scarcity of Whale Food. - ... </i> *(1913,Old Whaling Days,154)* [NOTES: 1836 Capt. Greene Report in 1913 McNab Old Whaling Days 154]  
> **THOMSON** I observed the surface of the sea, ... was literally crowded for miles together with swarms of small bright-red fish, rather larger than good sized shrimps. I was told ... that no use is ever made of them, nor could I find that they had any name assigned to them but that of `whale's food', as the whales are said to live on them. *(1867,Twelve Years in Canterbury,29)*  
> **LANCASTER** The water ran rust-brown with the whale-food. *(1904,Sons o' Men,112,1905)*

### whale gun
  
noun.  
See: -  
[NOTES: aka whale-gun]  
> A sextant .. coil lead line, whale gun and 3 harpoons ... *(Jun 10, 1876,Otago Daily Times,4)*  
> The great sea-serpent has at last, according to the latest report, not only been caught, but killed by some American fishermen, who shot a bomb-lance from a whale-gun into the back of its neck. *(Aug 10, 1893,Feilding Star,2)*  
> Unfortunately, the Aurora had no whale gear on board, and without a whale-gun and harpoons it was useless to attempt to catch one, and the only conclusive test whether the whales were "right" or wrong could not, therefore, be made. *(Apr 25, 1912,Poverty Bay Herald,6)*  
> On Monday the British exploration ship Discovery II is to leave Dunedin for the Antarctic, where an important part of the scientific work to be carried out will have to do with research into the movements of whales, whale conservation, and the exercise of an oversight into the whaling industry, which has been threatened with virtual extinction as a result of unrestricted slaughter by modern whale gun and factory ship methods. *(Feb 12, 1938,Evening Post,10)*  
> <i>A Whale Gun</i>. [heading] ... Down at the whaling station at Kaikoura some time ago I saw the first breechloader ... manufactured in this country. This was a whaling gun used for harpooning `right' and humpback whales by the Johnson party ... The sight of this harpoon-gun ... set me thinking. *(Feb 10, 1920,Quick March,37)*

### whale lance
  
noun.  
> **McNAB** ... 1 case slops, 10 cwt. biscuit, ... 12 iron pots, 1 doz. whale lances, 2 jars turpentine, ... *(1913,Old Whaling Days,85)* [NOTES: 1830 Aug 25 Ship Caroline manifest in 1913 McNab Old Whaling Days 85]

### whale louse
  
noun.  
> **POLACK** The Barnacle, commonly known as the whale louse, insinuates itself behind the fins of the fish - a part most sensitive - and on most other portions of the body. *(1838,New Zealand  (Polack),2/407)*

### whale net
  
noun.  
> **MALCOLM** Whale nets were set from a conical rock to the mainland between Home Point and Bland Bay. *(1982,Where it all Began,194)*  
> Messrs Cook Bros.. anticipate that, with improvements made in their net from experience gained last season, they will secure a large number of whales. *(Feb 4, 1893,Northern Advocate,00)* [NOTES: Check pagination]

### whale rider
  
noun. the heroine of a Maori legend, now the subject of a NZ film  
> Did you know that Ngai Tahu are descended from the whale rider Paikea? *(Oct 2004,AA Directions,Summer 2004/33)*

### whale station
  
noun.  
See: whaling station  
> WHALE STATION ON TRAMWAY. *(May 31, 1886,Wanganui Herald,3)*  
> Having neither boat nor canoe at hand, some of the party set out for Aparima whale station, the nearest point at which a boat could be obtained. *(Mar 4, 1903,Otago Witness,12)*  
> **McKILLOP** Frequent attempts have been made at the whale stations (at which places he was even more dreaded than elsewhere) to poison him; ... *(1849,Reminiscences,41)*

### whale-back
  
noun.  
See: whale's back - geog feature  
[NOTES: aka whaleback; re ship - universal, see OID, archive]  
> The whaleback is like a huge Cornish boiler, the upper side flattened, and with stem and stern exactly like the under side of the blade of a shovel as it appears with the handle laid horizontally. *(Oct 13, 1891,Southland Times,2)*

### whalebird
  
noun. any of various genera of seabirds which follow ships for food-scraps - <i>Prion</i> spp. (the 1850 and 1862 cits are possibly Prions)  
See: also dove prion  
> A dove petrel.. was found at Taihape last week... The whale birds, as they are known by sailors, are always very thin and emaciated when blown ashore. *(Aug 1, 1918,Wanganui Chronicle,4)*  
> From the feathers and portion of the beak sent, I am of opinion that the bird is a whale bird (Prion turtur). *(May 13, 1897,Otago Witness,40)*  
> For instance, two distinct species of whale bird were found on two islands only sixteen miles apart. *(Mar 27, 1926,Evening Post,6)*  
> The stormy weather experienced about a fortnight ago took a heavy toll among the dainty little whale birds which frequent the seas around the New Zealand coast, as, according to reports, large numbers of speciments [sic] were cast up on the beach in the Manawatu district. *(Jul 9, 1932,Evening Post,11)*  
> The whalebird is a bird of the southern oceans and is known to nest at Stewart Island and the Chatham Islands. *(Jan 13, 1940,Evening Post,10)*  
> ...[sic] flights of whale birds & cape pigeon ...[sic] [on the trip to New Zealand]. *(Dec 2, 1850,Richmond-Atkinson Papers,1/73,1960)* [NOTES: (J.C. Richmond)]  
> **BEGG** [Lat. 42.32 S.] We caught a bird, called a 'Whale-bird', the smallest kind of sea-fowl to be found so far from land. It was of a bluish colour and measured 12 inches from tip to tip and about 6 inches from beak to tail. *(1960,Early Voyages to Otago,11)* [NOTES: 1859 Aug 10 A. C. Begg Diary (1960) 11]  
> Jones makes a push for Ch.Ch. by this evening ... I roade [sic] into a fligh [sic] of whalebirds on the coast. There must have been near a million. *(Nov 12, 1862,Chudleigh Diary,65,1950)*  
> **BUTLER** The great feature of the southern seas is the multitude of birds ... Huge albatrosses, molimorks (a smaller albatross), Cape hens, Cape pigeons, parsons, boobies, whale birds, mutton birds and many more, wheel continually about the ship's stern, ... *(1863,First Year in Canterbury Settlement,i/12)*  
> ... the whale-birds, too, made their homes in the deeper crevices [on Bounty Islands]. *(1891,TrNZI,23/519)*  
> Above them circled and dipped flocks of.. whale birds "making a cheerful noise." *(Nov 2, 1920,Otago Witness,54)*

### whalebird
  
noun.

### whaleboat
  
noun. a whale-chaser  
> **McNAB** ... being afraid of the consequences, he gave the natives who came on board the ship in a canoe off the East Cape a whaleboat to go on shore with another New Zealander, ... *(1908,Historical Records,1/299)* [NOTES: 1810 Mar 12 Capt. S.R. Chace Declaration in 1908 McNab Hist Records 1/299]  
> **ELDER** The next day I accompanied Mr. Kendall to Rangheehoo in the <i>Jefferson's </i>whaleboat, where I found Duaterra dangerously ill. This was a very distressing circumstance to me. *(1815,Marsden's Lieutenants,120,1934)*  
> **ELDER** `The following is a list of articles lost:- 1 whale boat, ... *(1934,Marsden's Lieutenants,89)* [NOTES: 1815 Aug 31 Captain Hovell quoted in Elder Marsden's Lieutenants (1934) 89]  
> **HOWARD** ... James Caddell informed me of his having picked up part of a wreck together with most part of a whale boat upon a beach directly opposite this reef. *(1940,Rakiura,343)* [NOTES: 1823 June 1 Capt.J.R.Kent <i>Mermaid</i> log in 1940 Howard Rakiura 343]  
> **STEWART** .. Sept 2nd early in the morning perceived our Whale Boat, she came along side with certain strange men ... *(Jan 8, 1834,Letter)* [NOTES: 1834 Jan 8 Stewart Letter in 1913 McNab Old Whaling Days 64]  
> **McNAB** The Entry Island natives took the canoes ... and a skiff and a whale boat belonging to the ship *(1913,Old Whaling Days,389)* [NOTES: 1831 Feb 7 William Brown Deposition re Brig<i> Elizabeth</i> in 1913 McNab Old Whaling Days 389]  
> ... Price ... had bought a small 4 oared Whale-boat ... *(1835,Boultbee Journal,61,1986)*  
> and two men repairing the Whale Boat. *(Mar 1, 1838,Piraki Log,67)*  
> **TAYLOR** We left this morning about eight in the whale boat, ... *(Apr 24, 1839,Journal,2/114)*  
> **F MATHEW** We went this morning in our own Whaleboat to the Island ... *(Mar 10, 1840,Journal)* [NOTES: 1840 Mar 10 F.Mathew Journal in 1940 Founding of NZ 75]  
> ... the chief ... came off with us [and the others] came off in two whale boats, *(Jun 28, 1840,GBPP,(No.311) 107)* [NOTES: 1840 June 28 Thomas Bunbury's Report in GBPP House of Commons 1841 (No.311) 107]  
> **WEEKES** The beach was strewed with the bones of whales, and many whaleboats were lying about. *(Mar 21, 1841,Journal)* [NOTES: 1841 Mar 21 Weekes Journal in 1940 Establishment of New Plymouth 38]  
> **SELWYN** [We] came to the house of a respectable English settler ... who lent us his whale-boat, in which we rowed ... across Wangaroa Harbour ... where we arrived at seven P.M. *(Aug 17, 1843,Journal)* [NOTES: 1843 Aug 17 Selwyn Journal in 1845 NZ Part II (Church in the Colonies VII) 11]  
> **POWER** ... I have been nearly drowned, having been upset in Captain Richards's whale boat in trying to cross the bar of the Wanganui river. *(1849,Sketches in NZ,108)*  
> Bought whale boat and paid John Hay for it ... [pounds] 20. *(Jan 16, 1850,Torlesse Papers,126,1958)*  
> FOUND, ... a FIVE-OARED WHALEBOAT. *(Apr 26, 1851,Lyttelton Times,4)*  
> We availed ourselves of Mr Whiteley's kind offer of his whale boat to go a little farther down the harbour. *(Feb 23, 1853,Richmond-Atkinson Papers,1/87,1960)* [NOTES: (H.R. Richmond)]  
> **MOSER** ... we landed through the breakers ... while a whaleboat that was out with us ... had a very narrow chance of being capsized ... *(1863,Mahoe Leaves,18)*  
> **HAAST** .. I decided to hire a small boat belonging to the storekeeper, described to me as a whaleboat, but which ... was only a dingy with a square stern. *(1879,Geology of the Provinces of Canterbury & Westland,160)*  
> **HOCKEN** According to instructions the whaleboat met the weary travellers at the head of the Otago, and in safety carried them down to the <i>Deborah's</i> side. *(1898,Contributions to the Early History of New Zealand,54)*  
> When the whaling parties [on Kapiti] were paid for their greasy work - so much a `lay' - there were lashings of rum ... and for a few days the trying-out fires and the whale-boats had a rest. ... Card play ran high ... The stakes were usually thrown into a three-legged iron pot - the `kohua' of the old-timers ... *(Apr 11, 1921,Quick March,29)*  
> **COWAN** Three of us once cruised about [Stewart Island's] secret coves and in and out of its tidal creeks in a whaleboat carrying mainsail and jib ... *(1926,Travel in NZ,(Vol II South Island) 169)*  
> **HENDERSON** He also trained his crews and steered, winning altogether thirty-two first-class prizes for yachts, whale-boats, and other types. *(1948,Taina,vii)*  
> **LANGDALE-HUNT** We made it safely back to Pitt after a fairly long tow because the whaleboat was thirty feet long and it was a fairly stiff head wind. *(1985,Last Entail Male,127)*  
> **BAINES** I built her whale-boat fashion, twenty-nine feet over all, five feet eight inches beam, and two feet deep midships, sharp at both ends, the stem more bluff above the water-line than the bow. *(1874,Edward Crewe,129)*  
> **HENDERSON** Whaleboat men ... are not easily put out of action ... *(1948,Taina,4)*  
> **CRESSWELL** Sam Williams who worked for Rhodes had an accommodation house on the beach, Captain Cain lived in a cob house on the hill and superintended the whaleboat service, ... *(1951,Canterbury Tales,144)*

### whale-chaser
  
noun.  
[NOTES: aka whale chaser]  
> The Hawke and the Eagle are whale chasers, and are new vessels, launched this year, and at their trials they developed a speed of 13a knots. *(Mar 27, 1912,Poverty Bay Herald,2)*  
> Altogether the fleet consists of six vessels, the James Clark Ross, the parent ship, of 13,000 tons, a vessel which is known familiarly as "the factory," and there are five whale-chasers, named respectively The Star 1, 2, 3, 4 and 5. *(Apr 8, 1924,Hawera & Normanby Star,2)*  
> The Kos No. IV., one of the fleet of whale-chasers belonging to the factory ship Kosmos, arrived in Wellington yesterday, and is at present with the rest of the whale-chasers at Evans Bay. *(Mar 29, 1930,Evening Post,8)*  
> Two chocolate-coloured whale-chasers were lying still ... at the entrance to the [Queen Charlotte] Sound. *(1948,Our Own Country,166)*  
> **WILSON** A whale chaser used by Captain Harry Roderique to pick up birders on 7 June 1932. *(1979,Titi Heritage,74)*

### whalefeed
  
noun.  
[NOTES: aka whale-feed, whale feed caught by whitebaiters, sense two, empty headword]

### whalefeed
  
noun.  
[NOTES: the creatures caught by whitebaiters, sense two.   aka whale-feed, whale feed]  
> **McDOWALL** Fishermen on some West Coast rivers speak of "Dan-Doolin spawn" or "whalefeed"... Although whitebaiters know them by the name "whalefeed" they are in fact the young of several species of freshwater bully: the redfinned bully (<i>Gobiomorphus huttoni</i>), the bluegilled bully (<i>Gobiomorphus hubbsi</i>) and the common bully (<i>Gobiomorphus cotidianus</i>). *(1984,NZ Whitebait Book, The,21)*

### whalefeed
  
noun. Euphausiid crustacea  
[NOTES: one cit, no indication of species, archive (citation moved)]

### whalefeed
  
noun. <i>Grimothea gregaria</i>  
[NOTES: aka whale-feed, whale feed]  
> The basking shark, which grows to much greater size than the one found on Saturday is quite a harmless member of the family;  it feeds on small crustacea - the shrimps known as whale-feed. *(Apr 16, 1932,Evening Post,6)*  
> A SHOAL of the small shrimp-like marine animal, known as whale-feed, entered our harbor last week. *(Apr 2, 1872,Colonist (Nelson),2)*  
> **SENIOR** In the autumn ... they follow the `grit', or whale-feed, which is chiefly a small shrimp. *(1880,Travel & Trout,292)*  
> It is closely allied to the red crustacean or `whale-feed' (<i>Grimothea gregaria</i>), which is so abundant in Dunedin Harbour in the summer months. *(1898,TrNZI,30/580)*  
> Whale feed is very plentiful about the bays this year. *(Feb 26, 1924,Otago Witness,7)*

### whalefish
  
noun.  
> **PAUL et al.** [index to common names 283-293] whalefish, black 592 WHALEFISHES 591-2 *(1987,Fishes: A Bibliography)*  
> **AYLING** <i>BLACK WHALE FISH Gyrinomimus grahami</i> ... Whale fishes are named for the rounded whale-like outline of their bodies, not for their size; the New Zealand species is only 8cm long. *(1982,Collins Guide,132,1984)*

### whalefisher
  
noun.  
> **SELWYN** There is some truth in the saying, that the whalefishers impart a considerable amount of civilization to the natives. ... There is much, of course, in the habits of whalers which all must deeply lament; but I have rarely found a station in which advice was not patiently and even thankfully received. *(Aug 30, 1848,New Zealand (Selwyn),Part V (Church in the Colonies XX) 99)*  
> ... the Government might have founded a colony composed of stragglers from other settlements, of whale fishers and stock drivers ... *(Apr 19, 1851,Lyttelton Times,5)*

noun.  
> brought a coil [133] of whale-line, eight pairs shoes, six lances . *(Jun 8, 1842,Piraki Log,132)*

### whaler
  
noun. .  
[NOTES: whaling ship]  
> LOSS OF THE U.S. WHALER LION. *(Jan 2, 1857,Daily Southern Cross,2)*  
> The whalers Magellan Cloud and Gazelle are at Tonga:  the former has taken about 1500 barrels of oil. *(Aug 26, 1876,Auckland  Star,3)*  
> More likely they are men from missing whalers. *(Nov 10, 1881,Timaru Herald,2)*  
> The whaler Antarctic has sailed on a cruise, her eventual destination being Norway. *(Mar 25, 1854,Auckland  Star,4)*  
> Sing ho! for the wave-washed decks again / Of the good old wooden whaler, / That found on the shores of the southern main / A rest for the weary sailor. *(Dec 7, 1902,Otago Witness,59)*  
> **KENDALL** `Saturday, 25th. - Went on board the whalers, and also paid a visit to some natives ... *(Nov 25, 1815,Journal)* [NOTES: 1815 Nov 25 Kendall in Elder Marsden's Lieutenants (1934) 90]  
> We received every assistance from the manager, who dispatched a whaler the same night to bring round the remainder of the party. *(Jun 3, 1916,Northern Advocate,2`)*  
> Reference was made last week by AQE to the fact that it was busy direction-finding, and Mr. Goyder believes that the whalers now use wireless to enable the small boats sent out from the ships to ascertain the direction of the parent ship. *(Mar 31, 1927,Evening Post,20)*  
> The vessel is being ordered by the same financial interests as those for which Messrs. Workman, Clark built the giant whaler Kosmos, which made her maiden trip to the Antarctic last summer. *(Jun 28, 1930,Evening Post,10)*  
> In the early days of the war the Navy visited the Antarctic dependencies of the Falkland Islands and also destroyed oil installations in the South Shetlands which were used in peacetime by whalers. *(Feb 12, 1943,Evening Post,5)*  
> **McCRAE** ... we learnt his intention of proceeding to England in one of the South Sea Whalers then lying at Paroa in the Bay of Islands ... *(1820,Journal,13,1928)*  
> **MARSDEN** He was so far lost to all religious feelings when I was in New Zealand that he would contend that the civilization of the young women was promoted by their living as prostitutes on board the whalers. *(Mar 17, 1825,Letters & Journals of Samuel Marsden,415,1932)*  
> **WILLIAMS** At daylight observed the <i>Herald </i>working into the bay, and a Whaler, supposed to be the <i>Sisters. </i> *(Apr 7, 1827,Early Journals of Henry Williams, The,51,1961)*  
> **CRAIK** [Timber] had been brought to England by the Catherine whaler ... *(1830,New Zealanders, The,175)*  
> **EARLE** These men are called `Beach Rangers;' most of whom have deserted from, or have been turned out of whalers for crimes ... *(1832,Narrative of a Nine Months Residence in NZ,82,1966)*  
> ... disputes also among the English residents on shore and the crews of the whalers, are by no means unfrequent; ... *(1841,GBPP,108)* [NOTES: 1840 June 28 Thomas Bunbury's Report in GBPP House of Commons 1841 (No.311) 108]  
> ... disputes also among the English residents on shore and the crews of the whalers, are by no means unfrequent; ... *(1841,GBPP,108)* [NOTES: 1840 June 28 Thomas Bunbury's Report in GBPP House of Commons 1841 (No.311) 108]

### whaler
  
noun.

> **HAY** The Middle Island is frequented by the black whalers belonging to Van Diemen's Land ... fisheries. *(1832,Notices of NZ,136)*

noun. one who fishes for black whales

noun.

Natural History of Canterbury 192]

### whalers' Maori
  
noun.  
[NOTES: also whaler's Maori, whaler Maori]  
> **IRONSIDES** The language was a piebald mixture, generally known as "Whaler's Maori". *(1840,Journal)* [NOTES: >c1840-1841 Ironsides Journal in 1933 MacDonald Pages from the Past 130]  
> **THOMSON** Whalers, in their intercourse with each other, were guided by well-defined laws and customs; and intercourse between the races was conducted in a piebald language called Whaler's Maori, which was English embroidered with native words. *(1859,Story of New Zealand, The,1/294)*  
> **SENIOR** In this bay [sc Poverty] the whalers - the rollicking, hard-drinking, Maori-marrying fellows from Botany Bay, who ... invented a mixed language known as whalers' Maori - had important stations. *(1880,Travel & Trout,236)*  
> Otago is well-known as whaler Maori for Otakou, meaning "red earth," and approved by legislative wisdom, and not even the satire of the Hon. Mr. Mantell - O-tag-o, O-rag-o, O-bobtail-o, was able to prevent its adoption. *(Feb 8, 1909,Evening Post,8)*

### whalers' tea
  
noun. an infusion of manuka (tea tree) leaves  
> **SHORTLAND** ... as Mr Hughes and his [companion whalers at Onekakara] had long since drained their last can of grog, a capacious kettle of whalers' tea [128] stood on the hearth, ready for use of those who liked it. This tea, an infusion of `manuka' boughs, is a beverage much drunk by the whalers; it is very wholesome, and, not palatable at first, appears to be agreeable to those who have become accustomed to it. *(1851,Southern Districts of NZ,127)* [NOTES: [The above is paraphrased in 1913 Roberts Place Names of Otago 12] 1843 Nov 4 in 1851 Shortland Southern Districts 127-128]

### whalery
  
noun.  
> **BEATTIE** I never heard of him being manager of the whalery at Waikouaiti, but of one on the South Coast. *(1939,First White Boy Born in Otago,59)*

### whale's back
  
noun. a whale-shaped ridge of land; earlier form also <i>whale back</i> a hill-ridge (cf HOG(S)BACK)  
> **WILLIAMS & REEVES** Only think how much more graceful all the landscape will appear. / No more pigroots, whalebacks, hogbacks - these names that shall not last; / ... <i>Negro-heads</i> shall be re-christened. I for one will take no heed, / While to me there yet remaineth Negrohead, *(1889,Colonial Couplets,3)*  
> A low saddle (now known as Whale's back) ... *(1900,Canterbury Old & New,106)* [NOTES: Pratt, Canterbury Settlement]  
> **13** <i>Whale's back</i>.- A hill that rises steeply and slopes gradually down suggests a whale, and so there are hills and blocks of country in various parts of Canterbury called the W's.B. There was also an outstation of Hakataramea so called, and another at Highfield. (C.) the fragrant weed. *(Dec 30, 1933,Press, The)* [NOTES: Acland Glossary]  
> **BAKER**  *(1941,NZ Slang,59)* [NOTES: [quoting Acland 1933]]  
> It is to be hoped that the Nelson Government will make a road to connect the Amuri with this road, and thus avoid the present difficult and dangerous track over the "Whale's Back." *(Apr 27, 1861,Lyttelton Times,3)*  
> Finding there would not be sufficient water to risk a passage through the narrow channel below Black Jack's Point, Captain Ferguson deemed it prudent to return and take the Cross Channel, at the head of which the bank known as the Whale's Back was quite dry, a circumstance which seldom happens. *(Jul 14, 1870,Otago Daily Times,2)*

### whale-ship
  
noun.  
> **POLACK** The honours of the chieftainship are not confined to the aborigines; as some Englishmen, and natives of the South Sea Islands, who arrived formerly in New Zealand, as sailors on board whale-ships, have been enrolled as native patricians ... *(1840,Manners & Customs of the NZers,140)*  
> **ROSS** The whale-ships ... are now obliged to seek refreshments ... at some of the islands in the Polynesia group. *(1847,Voyage of Discovery to North Pacific Ocean,2/72)* [NOTES: 1841 Aug in 1847 Ross Voyage of Discovery 2/72]

### Whalewatch
  
noun.  
> Then, in 1987, local Maori set up Whalewatch, ferrying visitors out to view the sperm whales which visit the deep Kaikoura Trench offshore. *(May 2002,NZ Geographic,57/96)*

### whaling agreement
  
noun.  
> Brown called on his way from Akaroa, and signed the whaling agreement; also Capt. H *(May 21, 1842,Piraki Log,130,(ed. Anson 1911))*

### whaling gun
  
noun.  
See: -  
> <i>A Whale Gun.</i> [heading] ... Down at the whaling station at Kaikoura some time ago I saw the first breechloader ... manufactured in this country. This was a whaling gun used for harpooning `right' and humpback whales by the Johnson party ... The sight of this harpoon-gun ... set me thinking. *(Feb 10, 1920,Quick March,37)*

### whaling log
  
noun.  
> This is the finish of the Summers Log we now commence upon the Whaling Log. *(Apr 9, 1838,Piraki Log,69)* [NOTES: (ed. Anson 1911)]

### whaling party
  
noun.  
> The season for the Tory Channel whalers has officially closed with a catch of 107 whales.   This is equal to the previous record season, although a further two whales were caught that year after the official whaling party had been disbanded. *(Sep 1, 1945,65 Years of Guides)*  
> The whaleboat which was being brought from Auckland by the Wellington for the whaling party at the North Cape, was smashed by a sea. [sic] *(Jul 11, 1883,Auckland  Star,3)*  
> At Campbell Island a whaling party, with building material and a launch, were landed. *(Jan 27, 1909,Thames Star,4)*  
> During the recent whaling season Mr J. Perano's whaling party captured 34 whales, one of which was a valuable right whale. *(Dec 7, 1916,Feilding Star,2)*  
> Mr. J. A. Perano, head of the famous Tory Channel whaling party, and who was recently injured in an encounter with a fighting whale, was the central figure in a unique broadcasting arrangement last night, when from his bed in Picton Hospital he spoke over the radio telephone direct to the British Broadcasting Corporation in London, giving a brief history of the Tory Channel whaling industry and referring to some of the more notable adventures of his party and its predecessors in the pursuit of whales. *(Dec 12, 1934,Evening Post,12)*  
> When the whaling parties [on Kapiti] were paid for their greasy work - so much a `lay' - there were lashings of rum ... and for a few days the trying-out fires and the whale-boats had a rest. ... Card play ran high ... The stakes were usually thrown into a three-legged iron pot - the `kohua' of the old-timers ... *(Apr 11, 1921,Quick March,29)*  
> They protect it from southerly winds, while from the west, north, and east, shelter is afforded by the high land of Kapiti, the main land, and a small Island and reef, called Evans's from the name of a man who heads a whaling party there. *(9/5/184,New Zealand Gazette & Wellington Spectator,3)*  
> We are glad to learn that the prospects of our shore whaling parties are so good, and that those employed at the above stations have been so successful. *(Jul 7, 1853,Wellington Independent,2)*  
> The Waikouaiti paper states that Bradshaw's whaling party last season netted aGBP1,500 by their enterprise. *(May 14, 1873,Taranaki Herald,2)*

### whaling season
  
noun.  
[NOTES: includes qualified terms, eg the black whaling season]  
> The whaling season has been a failure at Norfolk Island. *(Jul 16, 1908,Star, The,3)*  
> The whaling season at Whangamama has been a successful one. *(Jul 16, 1908,Nelson Evening Mail,4)*  
> Although the whaling season is drawing to a close the first right whale was captured by the Te Awaite party in Cook Strait on Saturday last (says the Colonist). *(Sep 28, 1917,Evening Post,8)*  
> Although the whaling season is drawing to a close the first right whale was captured by the Te Awaite party in Cook Strait on Saturday last (says the Colonist). *(May 9, 1929,Evening Post,12)*  
> WHALING SEASON MUCH BETTER THAN LAST YEAR. *(Dec 31, 1930,Evening Post,10)*  
> The whaling season which ends on March 1 is a record. *(Feb 26, 1940,Evening Post,8)*  
> SAILED .. 15th, a small schooner, with supplies for Capt. Daniell's whaling station ... *(May 16, 1840,New Zealand Gazette & Wellington Spectator,2)*  
> The season for the Tory Channel whalers has officially closed with a catch of 107 whales.   This is equal to the previous record season, although a further two whales were caught that year after the official whaling party had been disbanded. *(Jul 25, 1840,Evening Post,3)*  
> The whaling season for the shore parties closed last month. *(Oct 8, 1853,Wellington Independent,3)*  
> The present whaling season is the worst in the Arctic for the last twenty years. *(Feb 17, 1869,Southland Times,3,3)*  
> The whaling season has so far proved successful that five good-sized whales have been caught. *(Sep 19, 1876,Otago Daily Times,2)*  
> The whaling season has been a failure at Norfolk Island. *(1/0/12,Star, The,3)*  
> The black whaling season in Hawk's [sic] Bay has been a very bad one, there having been but a few tuns of oil taken. *(Nov 7, 1854,Daily Southern Cross,2)*  
> **McNAB** The whaling season commences at Otago the latter end of March, during which whales are in abundance throughout the bay, and often caught within the harbour. *(1913,Old Whaling Days,178)* [NOTES: 1837 Jan 20 A.B. in <i>Australian</i> in 1913 McNab Old Whaling Days 178]  
> This day the Whaling Season ends, when the Boats were clear'd out, wash'd, oil'd, and taken on the Flat; likewise the Whaling Gear put up, Falls coiled away, &c. *(Oct 1, 1839,Piraki Log,99,ed. Anson 1911)*

### whaling station
  
noun.  
See: whale station  
> SAILED .. 15th, a small schooner, with supplies for Capt. Daniell's whaling station ... *(May 16, 1840,New Zealand Gazette & Wellington Spectator,2)*  
> He first established himself at Ikoraki, where for some years he had a whaling station ... *(Sep 26, 1877,Timaru Herald,4)*  
> A large number of whaling stations were etsablished [sic] along the coast of New Zealand as far back as 1825. *(Sep 28, 1881,Auckland  Star,2)*  
> On our way back to Lyttelton we touched at the Mahia, which was then a whaling station. *(Jun 8, 1901,Poverty Bay Herald,4)*  
> At this spot the old whaling station was situated, and the try pots are still to be seen. *(26/5/19,Wanganui Chronicle,9)*  
> With typical energy he threw himself into the merchant sphere of the colony, and, after severing his tie with Partridge, established a whaling station at Kapiti. *(Sep 15, 1941,Evening Post,8)*  
> **HEAPHY** A considerable quantity of it [hapuka] is cured at the whaling stations for winter provision, and is very fine. *(1842,New Zealand (various authors/titles),48)*  
> **ANGAS** Thoms's wife died ... and was buried at Te awa iti, ... on the opposite shores of the straits, where Thoms has another house and whaling station. *(1847,Savage Life,1/248)*  
> **WAKEFIELD** <i>Goats</i> were introduced ... at an early period, and are now in great abundance, especially at the whaling-stations. None have yet run wild in the country. *(1848,Handbook for New Zealand, The,170)*  
> **MOSER** Our work was but begun, and considering the examples that the whaling stations exhibited ..., we dreaded the introduction of more. *(1863,Mahoe Leaves,11)*  
> **HOCKEN** One night ... Dr. Shortland took up his quarters in a cask, probably an old whale oil barrell which had been washed ashore from some whaling station, and doubtless ... he enjoyed more peaceful slumbers than under the miserable shelter of the korari breakwind at Kuri Bush. *(1898,Contributions to the Early History of New Zealand,38)*  
> Whaling-stations for hunting humpbacks are now established at Whangamumu, near the Bay of Islands; Tory Channel, in the Marlborough Sounds district; and Kaikoura. *(1922,NZ Journal of Science & Technology,V/132)* [NOTES: (Oliver)]  
> **COWAN** In the South Bay ... is the most historic spot in <i>pakeha</i> Kaikoura, the whaling station, alongside the little native village of Te Hiku-o-te-Waero ... *(1926,Travel in NZ,(Vol II South Island) 23)*  
> **GUTHRIE** Later, whaling stations were established all along the coast ... *(1930,NZ Memories,11)*  
> **BEATTIE** Some of the whalers having been early on the coast and having Maori wives, their halfcaste children were mates of mine, but Waikouaiti being one of the later whaling stations, many of the halfcastes were younger than I. *(1939,First White Boy Born in Otago,24)*  
> **SLATTER** ... the <i>Tamahine</i> rounding Dieffenbach Point and out past the whaling station at Te Awaiti and rolling in the swell across the Strait. *(1959,Gun in My Hand, A,171)*  
> **JOHNSTON** At the beginning of the year was appointed to Te Awaiti, the whaling station. *(1968,Turn of the Tide, The,12)*  
> **FORDYCE & MACLEHN** In 1844 the Frasers had three ships and 30 men working the whaling station and 50 tuns of oil was collected in that year. *(2000,Bay, The : A History of Community at Titahi Bay,29)*

### whaling-ground
  
noun.  
[NOTES: aka whaling ground, whaling grounds, incl qualified phrases eg black whaling grounds]  
> Besides being situated in the very centre of the best sperm and black whaling grounds, New Zealand is within a few day's [sic] sail of the continental island of New Holland ... *(Aug 12, 1843,Daily Southern Cross,2)*  
> After leaving the islands the vessel made for the right whaling ground, but there they did not have much luck, only getting 130 barrels of oil and 1,020lbs of bone. *(Jun 25, 1887,Auckland  Star,8)*  
> The Christine has sailed for Russell, where she will ship her try-pots, and will thence shape a course for the "right" whaling grounds in the South Pacific, east of New Zealand, and due east of the East Cape, her eastern limit being 1,000 miles from the coast. *(Sep 5, 1891,Auckland  Star,4)*  
> RIGHT WHALING GROUND:  SOUTH PACIFIC. *(Feb 4, 1852,New Zealander, The,2)*  
> .. Port Nicholson is moreover nearer to the whaling ground, and would be the better staple place for the commodities derived from that business, one of the most important which can be carried on in New Zealand. *(Aug 1, 1840,New Zealand Gazette & Wellington Spectator,2)*  
> [February] 15, American whaling ship Orion, 400, Ray, for the whaling ground. *(Feb 23, 1850,Nelson Examiner and New Zealand Chronicle,23)*  
> Illinois, American whaleship, 413 tons, Potter, from whaling grounds (38 months out), with 1,000 barrels sperm oil, 1,100 barrels whale oil, and 9,000 lbs bone. *(Feb 28, 1863,Daily Southern Cross,12)*  
> June 11 - Milton, whaling barque, Fuller, for whaling ground. *(Jun 13, 1874,Auckland  Star,2)*  
> The mishap occurred on the whaling ground off the Chatham Islands on Dec. 4, while heaving in a whale. *(Dec 13, 1882,Star, The,2)*  
> A Norwegian whaler has started on an attempt to discover a whaling ground in the vicinity of Victoria Land in the Arctic Ocean. *(Oct 25, 1893,Poverty Bay Herald,2)*  
> The most successful whaling ground just now is the South Atlantic, in the neighbourhood of South Georgia, the South Shetlands, and the South Orkneys. *(Apr 22, 1919,Wanganui Chronicle,5)*  
> The Antarctic whaling firms say that it is impossible to exterminate whales there as long as hunting is confined to the recognised whaling ground. *(Feb 17, 1928,Evening Post,15)*  
> The whaling factory ship Sir James Clark Ross will leave Wellington at 6.30 o'clock to-morrow morning for Stewart Island to join her three chasers, and then proceeds to the Ross Sea whaling ground. *(Oct 10, 1930,Evening Post,11)*  
> **DIEFFENBACH** During the six remaining months of the year the ships cruising in the `whaling-ground' fall in with many whales. This whaling-ground extends from the Chatham Islands to the eastward of the northern island of New Zealand, and from thence to Norfolk Island. It is curious that the whalers assert that this whaling-ground is nothing but a shoal, although I am not aware that soundings have ever been obtained. *(1843,Travels in NZ,1/46)*

### whanake
  
noun. cabbage tree  
> **SHORTLAND** This was also the season for digging the `ti' or `whanake', which grows in great plenty and vigour near the base of the mountains forming the western boundary of the plain. <i>Ibid.</i> 3143 Ti, in North Island commonly called Whanake <i>(cordyline ti),;a liliaceous plant, resembling in appearance the Xanthorrhaea, or grass-tree of Australia. </i> *(1851,Southern Districts of NZ,230)*  
> **THOMSON** The tap-roots of the ti or whanake (<i>Cordylline australis</i>) are eaten for the sugar they contain. *(1859,Story of New Zealand, The,1/157)*  
> **HOOKER** Whanako. <i>Cordyline australis. </i> *(1867,Handbook NZ Flora (List of Names),769)*  
> **BAINES** The natives often snare these birds [pigeons], particularly when they are feeding on the `Wanaki', a kind of cabbage-tree palm. *(1874,Edward Crewe,45)*  
> **COWAN** In the rear of the church surrounded by lines of <i>whanake </i>or cabbage-trees (these <i>whanake,</i> now grown to enormous trees, still adorn the old village-site), was the <i>kainga</i> Te Reinga, ... *(1922,NZ Wars, The,1/353)*  
> **FULLER** Ti-kouka and whanake are varieties of the ti and there are many others. *(1978,Maori Food and Cookery,6)*

### whananga
  
noun. [Maori] traveller  
[NOTES: whananga: company of travellers (Ryan)]  
> Labour had a big job to do in areas like Northland, encouraging "more of my whanangas (travellers) to come back home to Labour from the Maori side and show provincial New Zealand that, hey, we do care about everyone". *(Sep 21, 2005,Dominion Post,A2)*

### whanau
  
noun. [Maori] an extended family  
[NOTES: Ryan 314]  
> The musical of Once Were Warriors is about to tour the country... Moriarty embraces the whanau. "There are two things going on here. There is us preparing to present what arguably is the biggest and first indigenous Nerw Zealand musical drama ever..." *(Feb 1, 2004,Sunday (Sunday Star Times Magazine),4)*  
> So is the obvious influence of <i>Bread</i>; the North England whanau is bread-and-dripping for First's storylines. *(Dec 17, 1990,Listener (New Zealand Listener),46)*  
> [Hdg] A voyage around my whanau. *(Nov 7, 2004,Sunday Star Times,C9)*  
> [Hdg] Whanau supervision rejected. *(Nov 20, 1999,Dominion,7)*  
> Remember how she started out? A  rural beneficiary with a big whanau. *(Jun 12, 1999,Listener (New Zealand Listener),14)*  
> The army is very much a Maori thing, I reckon. It's a natural place for us because it's about being a warrior and having that group camaraderie, the whole whanau thing. *(Oct 21, 2001,Sunday Star Times,C1)*  
> And yet - and here's the weird part - a hostel boy belonged. He had whanau. *(Apr 2000,North & South,130)*  
> Cups of tea with Jenny; Maxy-boy promising me nice boat rides, and "bring the whanau". *(Dec 26, 1998,Listener (New Zealand Listener),19)*  
> You get the impression that, for Pekepo, growing up with the Mongrel Mob was just like having one huge extended whanau. *(Nov 2, 2003,Sunday (Sunday Star Times Magazine),38)*  
> I can usually ring one of the whanau within 10 days of the game and be assured of getting tickets at a relatively cheap price. *(Jul 5, 2000,Capital Times,4)*  
> See, this is the problem where we get all hung up and say, 'That's a member of our whanau.' *(Mar 17, 2001,Listener (New Zealand Listener),21)*  
> Just outside you can hear the Harawene whanau shyacking on Christmas Day as it lines up its families for commemorative photos. *(Apr 23, 1993,Evening Post,5)*  
> I gave myself two reasons to proceed. 1. It was me - I was practically whanau. *(Apr 15, 2000,Listener (New Zealand Listener),58)*  
> As the story illustrates, land can also become a bone of contention, as the whanau gear up to make the most of the tourist invasion during the millennium celebrations. *(Jul 7, 2001,Listener (New Zealand Listener),60)*  
> But it is still sadly tainted by unacceptable levels of social decay and whanau dysfunction. *(Jul 22, 2005,Whakatane Beacon,2)*  
> **BUCK** The smallest sociological unit is the biological family, which the Maoris termed <i>whanau,</i> derived from whanau, to give birth .. The term hapu (pregnancy) was used to denote this expanded family group for it expressed the idea of birth from common ancestors .. The term <i>iwi </i> (bone) was brought into current use to include all the hapu descended from common ancestors and thus related to each other by a blood tie. To denote the groupings in English, the <i>iwi </i> has been termed tribe and the <i>hapu </i> a sub-tribe. *(1949,Coming of the Maori, The,333)*  
> **SINCLAIR** The tribe was an extended kinship organisation made up of sub-tribes and whanau, family groups larger than the European family. <i> Ibid.</i> 302  [Maori glossary] <i>whanaus </i> Offspring;extended family group *(1959,History of New Zealand,20)*  
> Anyway, she used to say, what with all my haddit kids and their haddit kids and all this haddit whanau being broke all the time .. how can I afford to buy a new house? *(1972,Some Other Country,159)* [NOTES: 1972 Ihimaera `Game of Cards' in 1984 Some Other Country 159]  
> **WEBSTER** The hapu were in turn composed of recognisable extended family groups known as hapu. *(1979,Rua,85)* [NOTES: [check double occurrence of hapu?]]  
> Mr Puketetapu described it as a good balance of the Maori family (whanau) approach encompassing cultural values, and the opportunity for members of the public to buy shares and become part of a New Zealand venture .. Two types of shares [in the Maori International company] will be available. The `Whanau' or group package shares, resaleable only to the company to ensure it retains control, will be available to Maori groups with a small percentage offered to members of the public. *(Dec 12, 1983,Dominion,5)*  
> **HULME** Whanau = extended family group - a general term for `family' now *(1983,Bone People, The,469. (406))*  
> **CORBALLIS & GARRETT** The idea of the whanau is demonstrated in another way too, ... *(1984,Witi Ihimaera,39)*  
> **HOWARD** Whanau - whanau is family, and whanaungatanga refers to the unifying force which binds families together, and thus the whole tribal group. The worst thing that can happen to a Maori is to be ostracised by his/her family group. Whanau refers to more than the nuclear family - the extended family of Aunts and Uncles have rights and responsibilities over a child - `whaea' can mean mother and aunt. *(1985,Towards Taha Maori,24)*  
> Mr Antonovich said the centre was a whanau or family for up to 150 people. `It was a melting pot of all sorts of people, including former Black Power and Mongrel Mob members.' *(Aug 24, 1986,Sunday Star (Auckland),A1)*  
> <i>whanau n. NZ</i> a Maori extended family. [M] *(1986,NZ Pocket Oxford Dictionary,877)* [NOTES: [nf. 1982 Collins Concise Eng. Dict.]]  
> Whanau (the extended family) and hapu (the functioning tribal unit) are the basic concerns of Maori history. *(Apr 1987,NZ Journal of History,17)* [NOTES: Binney]  
> To Mr Fairbrother, she said that with `the breakdown of the traditional whanau, many younger Maoris gravitated towards each other for warmth'. *(Sep 9, 1987,Dominion,8)*  
> Mr Ngata was from the Whanau-a-karuwai, Whanau-a-Rakairoa and Te Aitanga-a-Materoa hapu (sub-tribe) of Ngati Porou. *(Feb 1, 1989,Gisborne Herald,1)*  
> **MAKARETI** These families began as a man with his wife and children. When their children got married and had children, they would call themselves a whanau, or family group. *(1938,Old-Time Maori,34)*  
> The whanau has just bought some old second-hand machines so the kids can not only learn to sew but also provide sweatshirts to sell in the second-hand shop, started by the women to pay for their community work. *(Dec 6, 1986,Listener (New Zealand Listener),21)*  
> She spoke recently of the potency of a whanau-based values system "that had determined principles, knowledge, laws and practices to guide and nurture our development." *(Nov 19, 2000,Sunday Star Times,C4)*  
> **McCAULEY** Kevin had come up with the exhausting idea of taking everyone miles out towards the coast to meet his whanau. *(1990,Bad Music,46)*  
> Traditionally, the whanau was an extended family which incorporated three generations. At the head of the whanau were the kaumaatua (male elder) and kuia (female elder). Their whanau (offspring) lived with them or nearby. These included their children, adult children and spouses, and their grandchildren. Sometimes aged siblings of the kaumaatua and his wife were also included. This meant that a whanau could number up to 20 or 30 members. *(Feb 13, 1988,Listener (New Zealand Listener),69)* [NOTES: Ranginui Walker]  
> For those without whanau, the original institution is occasionally replaced by a quasi-whanau of a non-kin support group. In the western districts of Auckland there is a group known as Te Whanau o Waipareira. Now, since Waipareira is a district and not an ancestor, the term is a misnomer. The group is also much larger than the conventional whanau because of its open-ended nature. It is capable of taking in all those who live in the district and wish to belong. *(Feb 13, 1988,Listener (New Zealand Listener),69)*

### whanau
  
adjective.  
[NOTES: born, or reborn]  
> Te Kooti has gone to Awahuri with his following.   He will remain there until the 15th instant.   The Maoris say he is now whanau (born) and can be seen by visitors. *(Jan 6, 1893,Evening Post,3)*

### whanau
  
adjective.  
[NOTES: [Ma. <b>whanau (-tia)</b> extended family] cf. P.M. Ryan Dict. Modern Maori (1995) 314.]  
> I'd been been telling jokes at the whanau do, so I was keen to see how good these others were. *(Aug 15, 2006,NZ Herald - The College Herald,E2)*  
> The school surveyed all students during whanau time and analysed the results... *(Sep 23, 2005,Whakatane Beacon,4)*  
> <B>AGENDA</b> ..Annual report -Chairperson -Audit Report -Economic Development -Whanau Development... *(Aug 17, 2005,Whakatane Beacon,11)*  
> Because when something's a whanau matter, it's supposed to be left to the whanau to sort out - unless you want to be accused of paternalistic colonial oppression and usurpation of cultural imperatives. *(Jul 21, 2001,Listener (New Zealand Listener),17)*  
> Open plan schools .. are able to put into practice many of the family values of taha Maori in what is sometimes termed a whanau grouping. *(Sep 1986,Metro,68)*

### whanau
  
noun. [Maori] an extended family, esp. Maori

### whanau
  
verb. [Maori]  
> A Maori woman said several times that when she was a child and her mother was ill, she was "whanau'd" to her grandmother. *(Mar 3, 2004,Dominion Post,B7)*  
> A speaker on a local Maori radio station... told listeners that he had been <i>whanau-ed</i> as a child... The verb <i>to whanau</i> will be instantly accessible to most people, meaning the speaker's upbringing had been enriched by the input from uncles and aunts, grandparents, cousins and second cousins, an advantage rarely accorded to a non-Maori child. *(Jul 8, 2000,Dominion,20)*

### whanau centre
  
noun. [Maori]  
> A few months ago, the family services workers at the Kelvin Rd Whanau Centre in Papakura had a call from the principal of the school next door... But as they know only too well at the whanau centre, it's never that easy. *(Jul 6, 2005,NZ Herald,A19)*

### whanau land
  
noun.  
> Eventually they would end up living on whanau land alongside the Tutaekuri River at Waiohiki on the outskirts of Taradale. *(Feb 2005,North & South,88)*

### whanau-based
  
adjective.  
> ... the board cancelled a contract with the Rev. Harvey Ruru to run a whanau-based skills programme for young, disadvantaged people. *(Aug 24, 1988,Press, The,3)*

### whanauness
  
noun. [blend] belonging to a family  
> .. and yet another [wood stick] saying 'in spite of continued efforts to disband our whanauness here we are battered, yet strong' ... *(Jun 27, 2007,Internet - see Notes,2)* [NOTES: http://taputeranga.maori.nz/content/blogcategory/3/5/6/24/]  
> The 'whanau-ness' between all Black people was profound ... *(Jun 2006,Tumai,76/12)*

### whanaunga
  
noun. [Maori] family members  
[NOTES: [Ma. <b>whanaunga</b> relative (by blood), kindred] cf. P.M. Ryan Dict. Modern Maori (1995) 314.]  
> Thank you to our whanaunga from Rotorua, Edgecumbe, Wellington and Te Araroa for all your support, and aroha. *(Aug 31, 2005,Whakatane Beacon,16)*  
> Do you have whanaunga who work with you in designing stuff?  Yes. I have whanaunga who I call on to help in the work room. *(2003,Te Mana  Taiohi,10,Spring)*  
> ".. But what the dominant culture needs to understand is the different viewpoint Maori have of whanaunga (kin) ..." *(Dec 11, 2005,Sunday Star Times,A6)*  
> **Ihimaera ed.** Ranginui Wilson and I went back a long way. Had grown up together and were whanaunga. *(2007,Get on the Waka,118)* [NOTES: (Kingi McKinnon)]  
> .. our whanaunga who are right there running government departments, hospitals and huge private businesses ... *(Jul 26, 2005,Wairarapa Midweek,32)*

### whanaungatanga
  
noun. [Maori] (family) relationship, kinship  
> Mahinarangi wrote hundreds of songs, most of them reflective of her life experiences and whanaungatanga. *(2008,Mana,85/9)*  
> .. but as we met during the following wananga the whanaungatanga with whanau was so strong ... *(Oct 2006,Pu Kaea,4)*  
> "In so much Maori art, one engages with customary concepts like whakapapa and whanaungatanga, so people get very protective," ... *(Dec 14, 2005,NZ Herald,B4)*  
> This joint application indicates a shared whanaungatanga while maintaining discrete management areas of Ngati Manuhiri, Ngati Rehua - Ngati Wai ki Aotea. *(Nov 21, 2006,NZ Herald,C7)*

### whangai
  
adjective. adopted, adoptive; foster  
> Always and forever: Raiha, Vicor, Koko, Niecey and Jordon, Bob, whangai children Mokopuna, Analeze, Jayson, Jamaine and Michael. *(Dec 5, 2005,NZ Herald,C7)*  
> Whangai daughter of the late Alex and Maria Ropiha and Whanau (Waipukurau). *(Dec 6, 2005,Dominion Post,C13)*  
> My whangai Mum spoke Maori to me all, the time. *(Dec 1998,Mana,49)*  
> Beloved son of the late Peter Ateha and Maggie maki Rehia, Whangai son of the late Sam and Piri Tahau. *(Oct 31, 2005,NZ Herald,C9)*  
> She was influential in empowering her whangai brother Sir Robert to lead the tribe's treaty claim. *(Aug 16, 2006,NZ Herald - The College Herald,A5)*  
> It was a lonely, confusing childhood - especially when her whangai parents banned her from visiting her natural parents. *(Dec 1996,Mana,No. 14 : 14)*  
> ... and his many Whangai children, grandchildren and great-grandchildren. *(Nov 22, 2005,NZ Herald,C8)*  
> "I call her my whangai daughter," Hoet says, using the Maori term for placing of children in the care of non-birth parents. *(Apr 30, 2005,Listener (New Zealand Listener),16)*  
> The pair have always run their home on what he describes as a "whangai" basis, where their children are encouraged to bring their friends home. *(Jul 29, 2003,Dominion Post,B5)*  
> Thanks to .. Jason, Mother's Whangai Kotiro, those who sent flowers ... *(May 12, 2006,NZ Herald,C9)*  
> Reports of the case reveal that [he] adopted Luke at birth through a Maori whangai process. *(Jun 16, 2003,Dominion Post,B4)*  
> THOMPSON, Raymond Justin .. whangai son and brother of Jason and the late Auntie Vera ... *(Jun 28, 2005,NZ Herald,C8)*  
> A whangai placement will often be arranged if a family member is infertile, or if parents are unable to cope with a large family. *(Apr 17, 2002,Dominion,12)*

### whangai
  
verb. [Maori] to adopt, foster; as be whangaied = to be fostered (out)  
[NOTES: cf. P.M. Ryan Dict. Modern Maori (1995) 315.]  
> The household was always full of people - eight children of her own.., the kids she and Tex whangaied and fostered, along with anyone else who needed a bed, kai and maybe a good talking to. *(Feb 1998,Mana,No. 20: 8)*  
> Tihi and Wiremu have also whangaied two boys. *(Feb 1998,Mana,No. 20: 27)*  
> Even though we were whangaied around the family when she wasn't well... *(Nov 12, 2003,Radio NZ / National Radio)*  
> There are some children who find out they have been whangai-ed and feel they have been given away. *(Apr 17, 2002,Dominion,12)*  
> Matthews, Louise Anne._ Dearly loved whangied [sic] daughter, sister, aunty of Jeanne, Paki deLamare and Paki and deLamare whanaus at Mary Potter Hospice. *(May 1, 2008,Dominion Post,D9)*

### whangai
  
noun. [Maori] Maori customary adoption; an adopted or foster child  
[NOTES: 2 senses illustrated below]  
> I suggest that the churches could lead the way by embracing the Maori concept of open adoption called "Whangai" (pronounced "farnai"), which we saw in the Baby Kahu case .. "Whangai" could be a sensitive matchmaking partnership with school and parish. *(Nov 17, 2002,NZ Catholic,7)*  
> Retired academic Dame Joan Metge .. says the tradition of whangai (customary adoption) remains common. "It's still very much an integral part of Maori culture." Whangai or atawhai is a centuries-old custom in which a child is given to family members to raise. "It's something that is decided by the whole extended family group and it ensures the responsibilities for children are spread more widely" ... *(Apr 17, 2002,Dominion,12)*  
> In fact, this form of adoption - known by many tribes as whangai, but called atawhai in the Far North and taurima in Taranaki - is common in Maoridom. *(Apr 21, 2002,Sunday Star Times,A4)*
